115 年 10 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
10 月出口 967.6 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 10.9%，較上
年同月增 58.9%(按新臺幣計算增 66.6%)；累 計 1 至 10 月出口 7,582.9
億美元，較上年同期增 47.7%(按新臺幣計算增 51.1%)。 
（二）進口 
10 月進口 688.9 億美元，亦創歷年單月新高，較上月增 8.3%，較上
年同月增 75.1%(按新臺幣計算增 83.5%)；累 計 1 至 10 月進口 5,697.6
億美元，較上年同期增 44.6%(按新臺幣計算增 47.6%)。 
（三）出入超 
10 月出超 278.8 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 63.3 億美元；
累計 1 至 10 月出超 1,885.4 億美元，較上年同期增 689.7 億美元。

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
受惠於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及雲端服務等應用商機持續拓展，
加上適逢傳統外銷旺季，以及價格攀升效應，10 月出口較上年同月
增 358.7 億美元(+58.9%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 資通與視聽產品出口 476.0 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增
200.7 億美元(+72.9%)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增 151.6 億美元
(+67.5%)、電腦之零附件增 18.5 億美元(+1.0 倍)、儲存媒體增 17.3
億美元(+2.1 倍)、交換器及路由器增 11.7 億美元(+1.3 倍)。 
2. 電子零組件出口 326.8 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增 124.3
億美元(+61.4%)，其中積體電路增 120.5 億美元(+63.0%)、印刷電
路增 1.9 億美元(+38.5%)、電容器及電阻器增 1.2 億美元(+48.6%)。 
3. 基本金屬及其製品出口 28.8 億美元，較上年同月增 7.6 億美元
(+35.7%)，其中銅及其製品增 4.7 億美元(+1.2 倍)、鋼鐵及其製品
增 1.9 億美元(+16.4%)。 
4. 機械出口 28.8 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增 7.5 億美元
(+35.5%)，其中生產半導體等機械增 2.9 億美元(+57.5%)、機器、
工廠或實驗室設備增 1.4 億美元(+70.0%)。 
5. 礦產品出口 15.5 億美元，較上年同月增 6.5 億美元(+71.6%)，其
中石油煉製品增 5.7 億美元(+72.2%)。

（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月出口較上年同期增 2,448.3 億美元(+47.7%)，以資通
與視聽產品增 1,423.3 億美元(+72.5%)、電子零組件增 850.5 億美元
(+47.5%)增加較多，礦產品、機械亦各增 41.9 億美元(+36.8%)、41.5
億美元(+19.8%)。

生產等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
隨 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求之帶動，積體電路、資通產品
及半導體設備進口持續強勁，加上國際原物料行情居高，10 月總進
口較上年同月增 295.5 億美元(+75.1%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口 458.3 億美元，較上年同月增 195.0 億美元(+74.0%)，
其中電子零組件增 136.1 億美元(+1.1 倍)、礦產品增 22.8 億美元
(+58.0%)、電腦之零附件增 16.2 億美元(+70.0%)、基本金屬增 6.2
億美元(+33.4%)、塑化原料增 5.0 億美元(+22.0%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 163.4 億美元，較上年同月增 84.1 億美元(+1.1 倍)，
其中資通設備增 46.9 億美元(+2.0 倍)、半導體設備增 22.4 億美元
(+83.4%)、交通運輸設備增 7.6 億美元(+7.0 倍)。 
3. 消費品進口 61.1 億美元，較上年同月增 18.8 億美元(+44.5%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增 16.0 億美元(+5.3 倍)、食品增 0.5 億
美元(+5.1%)、小客車增 0.4 億美元(+10.2%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
10 月對美國、歐洲、日本出口創新高 
1. 對美國出口 329.3 億美元，較上年同月增 118.1 億美元(+55.9%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 98.3 億美元(+56.6%)、電子零組件增 13.5
億美元(+1.7 倍)、機械增 2.4 億美元(+41.6%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 204.4 億美元，較上年同月增 61.4 億美元
(+42.9%)，其中電子零組件增 47.4 億美元(+48.0%)、資通與視聽
產品增 5.6 億美元(+36.5%)、基本金屬及其製品增 3.2 億美元
(+95.2%)。 
3. 對東協出口 184.6 億美元，較上年同月增 84.1 億美元(+83.8%)，
其中電子零組件增 38.6 億美元(+75.5%)、資通與視聽產品增 36.5
億美元(+1.4 倍)、礦產品增 5.0 億美元(+2.4 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 59.5 億美元，較上年同月增 18.7 億美元(+46.0%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 10.7 億美元(+47.9%)、電子零組件增 3.3 億
美元(+81.3%)、機械增 1.2 億美元(+50.8%)。 
5. 對日本出口 36.7 億美元，較上年同月增 12.2 億美元(+49.6%)，其
中電子零組件增 7.5 億美元(+63.2%)、資通與視聽產品增 3.2 億美
元(+1.3 倍)、化學品增 0.5 億美元(+47.0%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月對美國出口 2,407.1 億美元，較上年同期增 891.1 億美
元(+58.8%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,805.3 億美元，增 416.6 億美
元(+30.0%)，對東協出口 1,524.1 億美元，增 547.6 億美元(+56.1%)，
對歐洲出口 481.3 億美元，增 157.1 億美元(+48.5%)，對日本出口 314.4
億美元，增 70.5 億美元(+28.9%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
10 月自東協進口 134.1 億美元，較上年同月增 82.3 億美元(+1.6 倍)，
自南韓進口 130.0 億 美 元，增 73.1 億美元(+1.3 倍)，自中國大陸與香
港進口 128.3 億美元，增 51.9 億美元(+67.9%)，自日本進口 62.0 億
美元，增 18.8 億美元(+43.4%)，自美國進口 53.6 億美元，增 14.7 億
美元(+37.9%)，自歐洲進口 48.2 億美元，增 9.5 億美元(+24.6%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較，自東協增 85.6%、
自南韓增 72.7%、自中國大陸與香港增 45.3%、自日本增 27.4%、自
美國增 23.0%、自歐洲增 12.1%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）今年 1-9 月主要國家/地區出口均呈上升，其中南韓(1-10 月)、香港、
新加坡受 AI 相關需求帶動科技供應鏈及區域貿易活動熱絡，累計出
口分別年增 60.6%、52.3%、41.3%；中國大陸、美國各年增 21.3%
及 14.3%，德國(1-8 月)、日本各增 8.8%、8.3%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步成長，累計 1-9 月以香港進口年增 51.8%較
高，新加坡、中國大陸、南 韓 (1-10 月)分別年增 39.0%、27.5%及 21.4%，
德國(1-8 月)、日本、美國各增 8.4%、8.2%、6.7%。

### Page 7

7
圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）隨全球人工智慧(AI)基礎建設投資強勁成長，帶動相關供應鏈需求擴
增，加以外銷產品價格攀升，10 月出口達 967.6 億美元，首度突破
900 億美元，年增 58.9%，連續 36 個月正成長；進口 688.9 億美元，
同為歷年單月最高，年增 75.1%，主要受惠於 AI 產業之國際分工與
出口引申需求增加，以及國際原物料行情高漲；出超 278.8 億美元亦
創新高。累計 1 至 10 月出口年增 47.7%、進口增 44.6%。
（二）10 月出口主要貨品中，資通與視聽產品、電子零組件出口值雙創單月
新高，各年增 72.9%及 61.4%，主因 AI、高效能運算及雲端服務等應
用商機熱絡；其餘貨類出口平均增 25.7%，其中礦產品因油價支撐增
71.6%，機械、電機產品受惠於半導體業者擴充產能及 AI 需求外溢，
增幅皆達 3 成，基本金屬及其製品亦增 35.7%。累 計 1 至 10 月資通與
視聽產品、電子零組件合占總出口比重約 8 成，年增 60.6%。

（三）10 月對主要市場出口年增率均逾 4 成，以對東協增 83.8%較突出，
對美國、日本、歐洲出口規模皆為單月新高，各增 55.9%、49.6%及
46.0%，對中國大陸與香港增 42.9%。累 計 1 至 10 月對五大市場出口
增幅介於 28%~59%，其中對美國出口年增 58.8%、對東協增 56.1%，
占總出口比重各為 31.7%及 20.1%，分別寫下近 36 年同期及歷年同
期新高紀錄。
（四）展望未來，全球經濟仍面臨地緣政治衝突、美國貿易政策走向等不
確定因素，惟隨人工智慧應用逐漸擴散至推論、代理式 AI，且國際
雲端服務業者持續布建 AI 基礎設施，帶動半導體、伺服器等相關供
應鏈需求延續，加以科技新品相繼推 出、年底消費旺季來臨之挹注，
我國第 4 季出口可望維持強勁成長態勢。

## Document 023: 2026-11-09_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が政策金利を翌日物に変更_65c10ae5

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-09_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が政策金利を翌日物に変更_65c10ae5.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

中国が政策金利を翌日物に変更 
～銀行金利市場化の長い道のり～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 月岡直樹 
080-1069-6684 naoki.tsukioka@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 人民銀がリバースレポ翌日物の日常オペを開始。短期金利の誘導精度を高める狙い 
中国の中央銀行に当たる中国人民銀行（PBOC）が10月30日、市場に短期流動性を供給する「売り戻
し条件付き債券購入（リバースレポ）」翌日物の公開市場操作（オペ）を11月2日より毎営業日実施す
ると発表した。これまで原則として毎営業日実施していたリバースレポ7日物から切り替える。翌日物
のオペは固定金利、数量入札の方式で行い、その金利を政策金利と位置づける。インターバンク市場
のレポ取引では翌日物が大半を占めていることから、政策金利を7日物から翌日物に変更することで
期間のズレを解消し、短期市場金利の誘導精度を高める狙いがある。新たなオペ初日の金利は1.25％
であった（前営業日のリバースレポ7日物の金利は1.40％）。 
この動きは、PBOCの潘功勝行長（総裁）が10月19日の講演で明らかにしていた金融政策運営の改革
方針を実行に移したものである。潘氏は「銀行間債券担保付レポ翌日物金利（DR001）は事実上、中
央銀行の金融政策が調節する目標金利となっているが、それと同時に、中央銀行の公開市場操作の主
要な種類と主要な政策金利の期限はなお7日で、両者は完全には一致しない」ことを認めた上で、「適
切なタイミングで公開市場の主要な操作種類の期限を翌日物に切り替え、公開市場の翌日物レポ金利
を主要な政策金利とすることを検討している」と予告していた。この切り替えによって、PBOCの金融
政策運営は米連邦準備制度理事会（FRB）や日本銀行のそれに近づくことになる。 
さらに、潘氏は銀行貸出金利の改革を進めていることも明らかにした。最優遇貸出金利（LPR）を
参照して貸出金利を決定するこれまでの運用に加え、DR001などの短期市場金利を直接参照して金利
を決定する方法を試験的に導入したのである。今後、財務力のある企業は商業銀行と協議の上でDR001
もしくはLPRを参照した貸出金利を選択することができ、小規模企業や個人には引き続きLPRを参照
した金利を適用するとしている。経済誌『財新』によれば、2026年7月以降にDR001を参照した銀行貸
出がすでに全国で100件以上実施されているという。 
PBOCが新たな銀行貸出金利の決定メカニズムを模索する背景には、LPRが「最優遇」から乖離し、
貸出金利の実勢を十分に反映できていないことがあるとみられる。これは、2013年7月に下限規制が撤
廃される前の貸出基準金利が基準の70％まで引き下げを許容していた歴史的経緯から、信用力の高い
顧客にLPR以下の金利で貸出を実行する慣行が残っているためでもある。潘氏は改革を進めると同時
に、すでに実務的な役割を終えている貸出基準金利と預金基準金利を正式に廃止するとした。 
2026 年 11 月 9 日

### Page 2

2

■ 貸出基準金利から最優遇貸出金利へ移行。ただ、実勢金利は「最優遇」から乖離 
PBOCは2010年代以降、銀行金利の市場化改革を慎重に推し進めてきた（図表1）。改革以前はPBOC
が貸出基準金利と預金基準金利を直接コントロールし、商業銀行がその上下一定の範囲内で貸出・預
金金利を決定する制度となっていたが、PBOCは2013年7月に貸出金利の下限規制を撤廃し、同年10月
には全国銀行間コール市場センター（全国銀行間同業拆借中心。以下「コール市場センター」）を通
じて貸出基礎金利（旧LPR）の発表を開始した。旧LPRは、主要9行（のち10行）の報告銀行がコール
市場センターに貸出の最優遇金利を報告し、センター側がそのうち最高値と最低値を除いた報告金利
を報告銀行の貸出残高で加重平均して算出するものであった。 
PBOCは銀行貸出金利が市場原理に基づき決定されるメカニズムへの移行を意図していたが、PBOC
が引き続き基準金利を操作して利下げを行い、報告銀行も基準金利を強く意識して最優遇金利を報告
したため、旧LPRは貸出基準金利に張り付いて変動した。PBOCは「銀行が貸出を実行する際、多くが
なお貸出基準金利を参照して金利を決定し、特に一部の銀行が協調行動を通じて貸出基準金利の一定
図表1 銀行金利の市場化改革 
年月 No. 改革内容 
2004/10  銀行貸出金利の上限規制と銀行預金金利の下限規制を撤廃 
2013/7  ① 銀行貸出金利の下限規制を撤廃（撤廃前の下限は貸出基準金利の 70％） 
2013/10 ② 貸出基礎金利（旧 LPR）の発表を開始 
2015/10 ③ 銀行預金金利の上限規制を撤廃（撤廃前の上限は預金基準金利の 150％） 
2019/8 ④ 貸出市場報告金利（新 LPR）の発表を開始。中期貸出ファシリティ（MLF）金利を参照金利に採用 
2024/7 [1] レポおよびリバースレポ翌日物の臨時オペを開始（金利はリバースレポ 7 日物の▲0.2％と＋0.5％） 
 ⑤ リバースレポ 7 日物金利を LPR の参照金利に位置づけ 
2026/6 [2] レポおよびリバースレポ翌日物の臨時オペを変更（金利はリバースレポ 7 日物の±0.25％） 
2026/11 ⑥ リバースレポ翌日物の日常的なオペを開始。その金利を政策金利に位置づけ

### Page 3

3

倍率（例えば0.9倍）を隠れた下限金利として設定しており、市場金利が実体経済に伝達することを妨
げている」と指摘している。 
そこで、PBOCは2019年8月、貸出市場報告金利（新LPR）と呼ばれる新たなメカニズムを導入する。
中長期流動性の供給ツールである中期貸出ファシリティ（MLF）の金利を参照し、銀行がこれに資金
調達コストや市場需給、リスクプレミアムを反映した最優遇金利をコール市場センターに報告する方
法である。報告銀行は中小銀行を追加して18行（現在20行）まで拡大し、算出方法も加重平均から単
純平均に変更したことで、算出結果がウェイトの高い大手行の報告金利に引っ張られる傾向を排し、
指標としての包括性も高まった。この改革により、LPRは政策金利であるMLF1年物の金利と連動する
ようになったが、LPRが「最優遇」金利から乖離した状態は依然として解消されなかった。報告銀行
が旧LPRの時と同様にMLF金利を強く意識して金利を報告したためと考えられる。 
図表3は新LPRと実際の銀行貸出金利との関係を見たものである。LPRを下回る金利での貸出比率が
年々高まっていることが分かる。LPR1年物は2019年8月～2026年10月までの間に計▲1.25％Pt引き下げ
られているが、実勢金利は資金需要の低迷もあってそれ以上に下がっていることを示唆している。 
一方、銀行預金金利は2015年に上限規制を撤廃した後も、銀行間の過度な預金獲得競争を防ぐため
窓口指導や業界の自主規制の形をとった上限管理を維持し、2022年4月になって10年物国債利回りや
LPR1年物を参照して金利を決定するメカニズムを導入した。上限管理が廃止されたわけではないが、
預金金利は近年、市場金利の低下に伴って低下傾向にある。 
■ 市場化改革の成果を銀行の実際の金利設定行動に定着させられるかが今後の焦点に 
PBOCは、2019年頃までに政策金利をリバースレポ7日物金利（短期）と MLF（中長期）の 2本立てと
する金融政策運営の枠組みを整え、新LPRの導入でその枠組みを明確化した（月岡（2024）参照）。
2024年7月には、政策金利をリバースレポ7日物に一本化し、LPRの参照金利もMLFからこれに移行さ
せた。中長期金利を市場の決定に委ねる形へと歩を進めたのである。 
PBOCは同時に、誘導目標とする短期金利を一定の範囲内で安定させるため金利コリドー（回廊）の
図表3 新LPRと銀行貸出金利 図表4 金利コリドー

### Page 4

4

仕組みも整える（図表4）。2024年7月、流動性を吸収するレポと供給するリバースレポの翌日物の臨
時オペを開始し、レポ金利をリバースレポ7日物の▲0.2％Pt、リバースレポ金利を7日物の＋0.5％Ptに
設定することでコリドーの幅を狭めた。さらに、2026年6月には臨時オペの金利をそれぞれ▲0.25％Pt、
＋0.25％Ptに調整し、誘導目標とするDR001が臨時オペ金利の上下限を継続的に逸脱した場合にオペを
実施すると明確化した。リバースレポ翌日物の日常的なオペを実施する準備を整えた形である。 
このように、PBOCは政策金利による短期市場金利の誘導精度を高めるとともに、市場金利の変化を
実際の銀行貸出金利へと円滑に波及させるメカニズムの構築を進めてきた。今回、政策金利をリバー
スレポ翌日物金利に変更し、LPRを介さずにDR001を直接参照する貸出金利の決定方式を導入したこ
とは、一連の市場化改革における重要な節目と位置づけることができる。DR001参照型の金利は指標
金利としてのLPRを代替するものではなく、PBOCは両者を併存させながら金利波及経路を改善するア
プローチを続けるとみられる。今後は、市場化改革の成果を商業銀行の実際の金利設定行動に定着さ
せられるかが焦点となろう。

は否定～」Mizuho RT EXPRESS（7月4日）

抗議，進一步加深市場對政府財政整頓能力的疑慮。10 月 19 日法國 10 年期公債殖利率上升

並透過主權債評價影響銀行業資產負債表。後續須關注法國財政政策、歐洲主權債利差及銀行
融資成本變化。
市場展望

使科技股波動加劇，但先進製程與半導體設備需求仍具韌性，AI 中長期成長趨勢未改變。隨市
場焦點由成長題材轉向投資效益，企業營收與訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率的重要性提
升，具技術優勢、財務穩健及獲利能見度的企業相對有利，而高度依賴外部融資者宜審慎看待。

避險效果。儘管 Fed 貨幣政策路徑與能源價格增添利率不確定性，短天期債券易受政策利率
牽動，超長天期債券面臨財政赤字、國債供給及期限溢價等壓力，相較之下，中天期美債的風
險報酬較為均衡。信用債方面，投資等級企業基本面及現金流仍具韌性，後續仍可持續關注。

## Document 101: 2027见闻历，财经人的桌面必备“神历”

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 11

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

鴻海(2317 TT) Trading Buy

AI 平台世代交替，2027 展望樂觀，投資建議 Trading Buy。 
投資建議：展望 2027 年，Vera Rubin 將成為主要成長引擎，主
要客戶對於 AI 伺服器的需求維持強勁，因此我們給予鴻海
Trading Buy 的投資建議，目標價 272 元。

### Page 2

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

2 群益投顧 
  ESG：   
環境：鴻海採用 TCFD 框架為基礎，全面盤點與評估氣候相關風險與機會，
並導入 ISO 14001 環境管理系統與 ISO 45001 職業安全衛生管理系統，同時
也推動「低碳供應鏈轉型推動計畫」，協助超過 30 家供應商完成節能專案，
累計減少碳排放約 15,000 公噸 CO₂e。鴻海已有超過 150 家供應商完成碳盤
查並簽署科學基礎減碳目標（SBTi），提升能源使用效率與減碳成效。  
社會：鴻海秉持「以人為本」理念，長期支持教育與青年培訓，設立「三創
育成基金會」，提供新創團隊資金與資源支持，自 2015 年以來已經培育超
過  100 組科技創業團隊，促進創新創業生態系發展，為社會培訓科技人才。
治理：董事會有設立「永續發展委員會」，主管由具備三年以上公開發行公
司財務主管經歷之財務長兼任，該委員自 2023 年起每年召開至少四次會議，
全體委員及相關主管亦於任職期間完成 ESG 專業訓練課程，強化永續治理
能力與資訊揭露透明度。 
公司簡介：  
鴻海精密工業股份有限公司（Hon Hai Precision Industry），成立於 1974 年，
由郭台銘創辦，總部位於新北市土城區。公司早期以模具及電子零組件製造
起家，逐步擴展至電子產品組裝、系統整合及設計製造服務，現為全球最大
電子專業製造服務（EMS）廠商。 
集團業務涵蓋消費智能產品、雲端網路產品、電腦終端產品及元件與其他產
品四大領域，旗下包含工業富聯（FII）、鴻騰精密（FIT）等重要子公司，
並透過全球事業單位及子公司分工，提供零組件、模組至整機系統的垂直整
合服務。集團生產及服務據點遍及全球逾 20 個國家及地區，包括中國、台
灣、印度、越南、墨西哥及美國等，全球員工人數於季節性高峰約達 100 萬
人。 
鴻海主要競爭對手包括廣達、緯創、和碩、仁寶及立訊精密等，依產品領域
有所不同。近年積極布局 AI 伺服器、電動車及半導體等新興業務，其中 AI
伺服器受惠全球雲端服務供應商（CSP）資本支出擴張，成為重要成長動能，
並持續鞏固全球電子製造服務龍頭地位。 
3Q26 營收 3.02 兆元，稅後 EPS 5.51 元： 3Q26 營收 3.02 兆元，QoQ+19%，
YoY+46%，毛利率 6.02%，QoQ-0.1ppts，YoY-0.33ppts，主要受產品組合及
原物料上漲影響導致毛利率下滑；營業利益 1,154.99 億元，QoQ+22%，
YoY+64%，營益率 3.83%， QoQ+0.07ppts， YoY+0.4ppts，營業利益成長高
於營收及營業毛利，顯現隨營運規模擴大，生產進入規模經濟；歸屬母公司
淨利 775.24 億元，QoQ+29%， YoY+34%，稅後 EPS 5.51 元。營業利益、淨
利和 EPS 皆創下歷史單季新高。 
10/2026 營收 MoM+12.7%，預估 4Q26 營收 QoQ+11.7%：鴻海 10/2026 營
收 1.31 兆元，MoM+12.7%， YoY+45.7%，創單月歷史新高，除受惠 GB300
持續放量外，iPhone 新機推出亦帶動營收顯著增長；Vera Rubin 僅小量出
貨。展望 4Q26，我們預期 GB200/300 出貨量季增持平至微幅下滑，Vera 
Rubin 則有雙位數以上的成長，並且由於 Vera Rubin 的 ASP 近乎倍增，預
估伺服器營收將季增雙位數。消費電子方面，我們認為記憶體與處理器成本

### Page 3

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

3 群益投顧 
上升，將使手機出貨面臨壓力，但產品 ASP 提升及 Apple 穩健的品牌效應
仍將為出貨量提供支撐，因此 4Q26 仍將受惠新機推出與傳統旺季帶來的成
長。相較於消費電子的季節性，GB200/300 放量與產品組合變化對全年營運
的影響更大，因此 2026 年的主要成長引擎仍是伺服器業務的擴張；Vera 
Rubin 則為 2027 年的成長建立基礎。綜上所述，群益預估 4Q26 營收 3.37
兆元，QoQ+11.7%；毛利率/營益率持續受 Vera Rubin 新平台出貨而稀釋，
下滑 0.10ppts/0.06ppts 來到 5.92%/3.77%，歸屬母公司淨利 899.02 億元，
QoQ+15.97%，稅後 EPS 6.39 元。 
雲端網路成為營運主軸，規模經濟支持本業獲利成長： 3Q26 雲端網路產品
占營收 54%，高於 2Q26 的 51%。消費智能、電腦終端及元件與其他產品分
別占 29%、12%及 5%。雲端網路產品受 AI 伺服器及機櫃出貨帶動，季增與
年增均強勁。消費智能產品亦受新品與傳統旺季帶動，季增優於公司原先預
期。據我們所知，伺服器約占雲端網路營收的 85%-90%，其中約 70%來自
AI 伺服器。隨雲端網路占比提高，AI 機櫃的交付時程、平台組合及物料認
列方式，將更直接影響鴻海的營收與獲利率。鴻海 3Q26 毛利率 6.02%，QoQ-
0.1ppts，主要受到整櫃 GPU 伺服器高昂的 BOM 所 稀 釋；營 業 利 益 率 3.83%，
QoQ+0.07ppts，雖然毛利率季減，營益率卻有所改善，反映出營收規模擴大
及營運效率提升的效益。 
Vera Rubin 接力 GB300，2027 年進入新平台主要貢獻期：AI 機櫃的新舊平
台交替是鴻海延續成長的核心動能。鴻海預計 2026 年 AI 伺服器機櫃出貨
較 2025 年倍增以上，且既有 GB 平台需求可能延續至 2027 年。此外，Vera 
Rubin 與 GB 系列同為 NVL72，因此我們預期鴻海將可較平滑的實現世代交
替。管理層預估 4Q26 Vera Rubin出貨量將呈高雙位數增長。展望 2027 年，
伺服器業務上會新增更多 Neocloud 客戶，但主要客戶仍然是大型 CSP。整
體訂單並未隨近期市場的擔憂而調整，對於 AI 投資的展望維持正向，預估
AI 機櫃出貨量將呈高雙位數成長。 
自製零組件與自動化支撐獲利，高 BOM 仍主導毛利率走勢：鴻海透過垂直
整合提高單一專案可承接的製造內容，管理層表示除規模經濟外，零組件自
製率的提高與自動化生產都將為毛利率及營益率提供支撐。根據我們調查，
在晶片以外的零組件自製率約 50%，布局包括連接器、線材、Midplane、交
換器及散熱相關產品，零組件毛利率高於 15%，部分甚至可達 20%，管理層
亦提出 800G 以上交換器全年營收倍增的展望，這些產品有助鴻海由機櫃組
裝向高速互聯及關鍵元件延伸，並可提升附加價值。然而據我們了解目前鴻
海在 GPU 的大型專案中尚未採用 consignment model，記憶體等元件漲價則
主要透過 cost-plus 模式轉嫁給客戶，進入 Vera Rubin 世代後，由於 ASP 將
近倍增，會進一步造成毛利率稀釋，考慮到 GPU Rack 單價極高，我們預估
2027 年毛利率仍將承壓。 
ASIC 拓展產品組合，物料模式與 ASP 差異使營收比重低於出貨量佔比：據
我們了解，鴻海 ASIC 平台約佔 AI 伺服器營收的 20%，較 1H26 略升，且
ASIC 平台成長速度高於 GPU 平台，除既有客戶外亦有新專案，管理層預估
3Q26 ASIC 伺服器出貨量 YoY+150%，高於 GPU 伺服器 70%的出貨量年增。
我們認為 ASIC 專案增加可擴大鴻海在不同運算平台的參與，減少成長來源
集中於單一 GPU 平台的程度。此外，大部分 ASIC 專案將有望採用
consignment model，有助於緩解 AI 伺服器對於整體毛利率的負面效果，然
而 GPU 平台 ASP 顯著高於 ASIC 平台，因此 ASIC 的營收較難完整反映其

### Page 4

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

4 群益投顧 
專案及製造規模。我們認為目前 ASIC 專案採用 consignment model 尚不足
以顯著改善 GPU Rack 高 BOM 對整體毛利率的稀釋。 
策略投資三菱電機，電動車可望成為未來另一成長引擎：鴻海透過與三菱電
機的策略結盟深化在電動車領域的發展，雖投資金額小於 AI 相關的支出，
但將持續投入研發，目前已做好充足準備量產，將尋機大規模進入 EV 市場，
可望成為未來的另一成長動能。 
預估 2027 年營收 15.1 兆元，稅後 EPS 24.71 元：我們預估隨 AI 機櫃放量
將提高營收與獲利規模，2027 年營收 15.1 兆元，YoY+37.1%，毛利率 5.65%，
YoY-0.4ppts；營業利益 5,181.73 億元，YoY+25.5%，營益率 3.43%，YoY-
0.3ppts；歸屬母公司淨利 3,478.27 億元，YoY+25.4%，稅後 EPS 24.71 元。
Vera Rubin 由初期出貨轉為全年營收貢獻，加上既有 GB300 需求延續及
ASIC 業務擴張，帶動全年營收顯著增長。公司持續布局台灣、美國、墨西
哥及越南產能，亦有助因應客戶在不同地區的交付需求。 
投資建議：受惠 AI 伺服器的強勁需求，2026 AI 機櫃出貨可望倍數增長。
此外我們預期憑藉 iPhone 的品牌效應，4Q26 智慧型消費電子業務可望維持
過往的傳統出貨旺季。展望 2027 年，Vera Rubin 將成為主要成長引擎，主
要客戶對於 AI 伺服器的需求依舊強勁。關鍵製程自動化及零組件自製率的
提升亦有助提升營運效率，為毛利率及營益率提供部分支撐。考量上述原
因，我們給予鴻海 Trading Buy 的投資建議，目標價 272 元。

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Update
MPI Corporation  | Asia Pacific
Potential further upside in
2027/28 capacity expansion;
stay OW
MPI hosted its 3Q26 earnings call on November 16. Key takeaways:
There could be further upside in 2027/28 capacity expansion: The company
reiterated that it would add at least 2.5mn pins for MEMS and 0.5mn pins for VPC in
2027. It may raise the numbers after its budget plan is finalized in December. MPI
expects to maintain aggressive capacity expansion in 2028, given strong demand.

2027 guidance appears conservative: The company guided for 2027 revenue to be
up at least 50% YoY with gross margin >60% –   conservative vs. consensus
expectations of 2027 revenue doubling. However, we expect MPI to beat its own
guidance  and raise it gradually, per previous practice of amendment.
In qualification for second insertion 2 customer: The company is  still in
qualification for insertion 2. However, it confirmed it has a second significant

customer; the schedule of the qualification could be similar to the first one. MPI said
it would be the only supplier for the first  customer's  insertion 2 process. CPO could
make up a double-digit share of 2028 total revenue.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
                        MPI Corporation (6223.TWO)                    
We derive our price target from a residual income model, as we believe this can best capture 
the long-term value. We assume a 9.78% cost of equity (risk-free rate 2.0% and risk premium 
6.0%), a payout ratio of 67%, a medium-term growth rate of 16.0%, and a terminal growth rate 
of 4.0%. Our price target implies 50x/25x 2027e/2028e P/E. We believe its valuation will con-
tinue to climb along with growing earnings. 
                        Risks to Upside                    
n Stronger-than-expected AI demand
n Stronger-than-expected market share gain from peers
n Stronger-than-expected CPO development

Risks to Downside                    
n Slower-than-expected AI demand
n Slower-than-expected market share gain from peers
n Slower-than-expected CPO development

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

## Document 229: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리 사이클SK하이닉스(000660) 높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리_0a1416f9

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리 사이클SK하이닉스(000660) 높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리_0a1416f9.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026년 10월 29일 
기업분석

SK하이닉스 (000660) 
높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리 사이클

Buy(유지) 
목표주가 3,150,000원, 현재 주가(10/28): 1,487,000원 
▶ Analyst 박준영 jyp94@hanwha.com 3772-7481 / RA 김나우 now.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7710

3Q26 Review: HBM4 출하 확대와 예상보다 강한 가격 상승 
분기 매출액 104.6조원(QoQ +32%, YoY +328%), 영업이익 83.3조원(QoQ +38%, YoY +632%)을 기록하며 시장 기대
치를 상회함. 영업이익률은 전분기 대비 약 3%p 상승한 80%로 사상 최대 분기 실적을 달성함. 특히 DRAM ASP가 전
분기 대비 20% 후반 상승하며 당사 기존 예상치인 +23%를 상회한 점에 주목할 필요가 있음. NAND ASP 역시 20% 
중반 상승하며 예상보다 강한 가격 흐름을 나타냄. DRAM ASP 상승에는 기존 DRAM의 가격 인상과 함께 3분기부터 
본격화된 HBM4 양산 및 출하 확대에 따른 제품 믹스 개선 효과가 반영됨. 서버 DDR5 판매량 역시 전분기 대비 40% 
증가하며 실적 성장을 뒷받침함. 특히 HBM4는 기존 HBM3E 대비 높은 기술적 난도를 반영한 가격으로 공급되고 있어 
향후 출하 비중 확대에 따른 추가적인 ASP 상승 효과도 기대됨. 
이번 실적에서 중요한 부분은 예상보다 높은 가격 상승폭이 2027년 실적 추정치의 상향 가능성을 높이고 있다는 점임. 3
분기 DRAM ASP가 기존 추정치를 상회한 가운데 4분기에도 HBM4 출하 확대와 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 이어질 
것으로 예상되기 때문임. 동사는 4분기 DRAM 출하량이 전분기 대비 20% 중반 증가할 것으로 전망했으며, HBM4 출
하량이 HBM3E를 넘어 주력 제품으로 자리 잡을 것으로 예상함. 특히 HBM4의 양산 수율이 당초 목표를 상회하고 있
으며 높은 고객 수요에 대응하기 위해 생산성 극대화에 집중하고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. HBM4 출하 확대에 따른 믹스 
개선과 기존 DRAM 가격 상승이 동시에 진행되는 가운데 HBM과 일반 DRAM의 수익성 격차 역시 유의미하게 축소
될 것으로 예상됨. 이는 특정 제품에 대한 수익성 의존도가 낮아지면서 DRAM 사업 전반의 이익 체력이 강화되고 있음
을 의미함. 메모리 산업의 높은 영업 레버리지를 고려하면 예상보다 높은 ASP와 출하량 증가는 2027년 이익 추정치의 
추가 상향으로 연결될 가능성이 높다고 판단함.

공급 부족의 장기화와 LTA 확대, 메모리 산업의 이익 구조 변화 
이번 컨퍼런스콜에서 확인된 또 다른 중요한 변화는 공급 부족의 장기화와 장기공급계약(LTA) 확대임. 동사는 첨단 공정
의 기술적 난도 상승과 신규 생산시설 구축에 소요되는 시간을 고려할 때 메모리 공급이 수요를 충족하지 못하는 환경이 
2030년까지 지속될 것으로 전망함. 특히 글로벌 주요 고객사 14곳과 장기공급계약을 체결했으며, 2027~2028년 전체 생
산능력의 50% 이상에 해당하는 물량을 확보함. HBM의 경우 2029년까지의 물량이 이미 판매 확정된 상황으로 중장기 
수요 가시성이 상당히 높아졌다고 판단함. 주목할 부분은 LTA가 단순한 공급물량 확보를 넘어 메모리 업체의 사업 구조
와 이익 안정성을 변화시키고 있다는 점임. 계약 가격은 고객 및 제품별로 차이가 있으나 합의된 범위 내에서 시장 상황
을 반영하는 구조로 운영되고 있으며 예치금 등 실질적인 계약 이행력을 높이는 장치도 포함됨. 이는 장기적인 판매물량
을 확보하면서도 업황 상승에 따른 가격 인상 효과를 일정 부분 누릴 수 있다는 점에서 긍정적임.

### Page 2

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

2 
특히 전체 생산능력의 50% 이상을 LTA로 확보하면서 나머지 물량은 시장 상황에 따라 탄력적으로 대응할 수 있다는 점
에 주목할 필요가 있음. 장기계약을 통해 안정적인 매출 기반을 확보하는 동시에 미계약 물량을 활용해 추가적인 가격 
상승에 대응할 수 있기 때문임. 물론 장기계약이 메모리 가격 하락을 완전히 방어하는 것은 아니지만, 과거와 비교해 물
량 가시성과 고객사와의 협상력이 동시에 개선되고 있다는 점은 분명함. 과거 메모리 산업이 높은 이익을 창출하는 국면
에서도 낮은 밸류에이션을 적용 받았던 이유 중 하나는 이익의 지속성에 대한 불확실성이었음. 그러나 장기계약 확대와 
공급 제약의 장기화가 동시에 진행된다면 시장이 평가하는 이익의 지속성 역시 높아질 수 있음. 따라서 향후 주가 상승
은 단순한 EPS 추정치 상향뿐 아니라 이익 변동성 축소에 따른 밸류에이션 재평가가 동시에 진행되는 형태로 나타날 가
능성이 높다고 판단함. 
AI 인프라 투자에 대한 우려 역시 중장기 메모리 수요를 훼손할 가능성은 제한적이라고 판단함. 동사는 주요 하이퍼스케
일러의 클라우드 매출이 전년 대비 30~80% 성장하고 있으며 기업용 AI 솔루션 계약 확대와 클라우드 수주잔고 증가를 
통해 AI 투자의 사업적 성과가 확인되고 있다고 설명함. 특히 초기 AI 투자가 소수 빅테크에 집중되었던 것과 달리 최근
에는 일반기업의 자체 인프라 구축, 소버린 AI, 온프레미스 등으로 투자 주체가 다변화되고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. 특정 
사업자의 투자 속도 조절이 전체 AI 인프라 수요에 미치는 영향은 과거 대비 완화될 수 있기 때문임. AI 투자에 대한 우
려가 지속되는 상황에서도 실제 메모리 수요와 고객사들의 장기계약 확대가 확인되고 있다는 점에서 메모리 업황의 구조
적 성장에 대한 긍정적인 시각을 유지함.

HBM4 경쟁력 강화와 CAPEX 확대, 이익 성장과 주주환원의 선순환 
HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대와 신규 생산능력 확보 역시 2027년 실적 성장의 중요한 기반이 될 전망임. 동사는 M15X
의 생산능력을 연말까지 최대 수준으로 확대할 계획이며 HBM4의 양산 수율 역시 당초 목표를 상회하는 등 안정적인 
생산체계를 확보하고 있음. 특히 HBM 수요가 공급능력의 두 배를 상회하는 상황에서 고객사들의 높은 선호가 이어지고 
있다는 점은 경쟁력 유지에 긍정적임. 최근 일부 고객사들이 HBM 적층 단수를 낮추는 방안을 검토하고 있으나 이는 공
급 부족 상황에서 제한된 물량을 효율적으로 활용하기 위한 전략으로 판단됨. 적층 단수 조정이 전체 HBM 수요 감소로 
직결되는 것은 아니며 오히려 후공정 생산능력에 대한 요구가 확대될 가능성도 존재함. HBM4E와 커스텀 HBM 개발 
역시 고객사 일정에 맞춰 진행되고 있어 차세대 제품에서도 경쟁력을 유지할 것으로 기대됨. 
한편 2027년 CAPEX는 선단 공정 전환과 용인 클러스터를 비롯한 국내외 생산 인프라 확충으로 전년 대비 증가할 전망
임. 다만 CAPEX 확대를 과거와 같은 공급 과잉의 신호로 해석할 필요는 없다고 판단함. 선단 공정의 기술적 난도 상승
과 고부가 제품 비중 확대에 따라 투자금액 증가가 실제 생산량 증가로 정비례하지 않기 때문임. 특히 동사는 신규 팹 
건설 일정을 앞당기면서도 클린룸은 순차적으로 개방하고 실제 생산능력 확대는 최종 수요와 수익성을 고려해 단계적으
로 진행할 계획임. 고객사의 투자 계획뿐 아니라 재무건전성과 자금조달 여력, 공급망 준비 상황까지 점검해 CAPEX 집
행에 반영한다는 점도 긍정적임. 이는 AI 인프라 투자 확대에 대응하면서도 과거 메모리 산업의 공급 과잉 위험을 최소
화하기 위한 전략으로 판단함. 
동사는 이에 더해 2026년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원 규모의 주주환원 계획을 발표했음. 현금배당 30조원 이상, 자사주 
매입 및 소각 60조원 이상으로 구성되며, 기존에 약속했던 잉여현금흐름(FCF)의 50% 이상을 주주에게 환원한다는 원칙
을 구체화한 것임. 지난 8월 발표한 40조원 규모의 자사주 매입은 10월 21일 완료됐으며, 해당 주식은 향후 소각될 예정
임. 이번 3분기 이사회에서는 현금배당 14조원과 추가 자사주 매입 20조원을 결의했으며, 자사주 매입액 중 10조원은 전
량 소각하고 나머지 10조원은 임직원 보상에 활용할 계획임. 임직원 보상용 자사주는 주주환원 규모에서 제외됨. 특히 
향후에도 자사주 매입을 지속할 계획이라는 점에서 주주환원 정책의 지속성이 강화됐다는 판단임.

### Page 3

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

3 
3분기 주당 배당금은 2만원으로, 실적 개선을 반영한 일반배당 1만원과 키옥시아 투자수익을 활용한 특별배당 1만원으
로 구성됨. 총 배당 규모는 약 14조원이며 회사는 올해 실현한 키옥시아 투자수익의 대부분을 3분기 및 연말 특별배당을 
통해 주주에게 환원할 계획임. 이번 결정은 메모리 가격 상승과 고부가 제품 확대에 따른 현금창출력 개선이 실질적인 
주주환원 확대로 연결되고 있음을 보여줌. 특히 2027년 설비투자 확대가 예상되는 상황에서도 대규모 현금배당과 자사
주 소각을 병행한다는 점은 재무적 여력에 대한 자신감을 반영하는 것으로 판단됨. 향후에도 안정적인 실적 성장과 FCF 
확대가 이어질 경우 주주환원의 지속 가능성이 높아질 전망이며 이는 과거 대비 높아진 이익 창출력과 함께 중장기 밸류
에이션 재평가를 뒷받침하는 요인으로 작용할 것으로 예상됨. 
결국 이번 실적발표에서 확인된 핵심은 예상보다 강한 메모리 가격 상승과 HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대, 그리고 장기계
약을 통한 이익 가시성 개선임. 특히 HBM4의 높은 가격과 안정적인 양산 수율, 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 동시에 확
인되면서 2027년 이익 추정치의 추가 상향 가능성이 높아졌다고 판단함. 여기에 LTA 확대에 따른 중장기 이익 안정성 
개선과 대규모 주주환원까지 더해지고 있음. 실적 추정치 상향과 이익의 지속성 개선이 동시에 진행되고 있다는 점에서 
긍정적인 투자 시각을 유지함.

이 자료는 조사분석 담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성됐음을 확인합니다. 
본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 자료를 
기관투자자 또는 제3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (박준영, 김나우) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 5

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

5 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 09 월 30 일) 
투자등급 매수 중립 매도 합계 
금융투자상품의 비중 96.4% 3.6% 0.0% 100.0%

자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

2027년 매출 191조(YoY +2%, 이하 YoY), OP 12.7조(+15%), OPM 6.6% 전망 
기저효과 바탕으로 연결 도매 물량 YoY +5% 증가 예상하나, 원달러 환율 -
4% 하락(가정 환율 1,380원)하며 1H27 → 2H27로가며 이익체력 개선 예상

자료: 현대차, 대신증권 Research Center

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

### Page 6

Q. 3Q26 법인세비용 증가 원인 및 4Q26 전망 
- 일회성 요인보다는 법인별 손익 믹스 및 적용 세율 차이에 따른 영향 
- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

- Overweight(비중확대)

- Neutral(중립) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상

- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상

  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 하락 예상

## Document 263: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(329180) 
HD 현대중공업 
2026.10.29

AI 로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 610,000 원(하향) 
 현재주가 285,500 원(10/28)

 3Q26 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2%yoy), 영업이익은 8,992억원
(+61.3%yoy, OPM 15.6%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센서스 9,792억원 하회.  
 평균 환율이 약 79원 하락하며 영업이익 약 450억원이 줄었고, 조업
일수는 47일로 통상 계절성 대비 4일 추가 감소해 약 500억원의 이
익 감소 효과 발생. 임단협 추가 비용의 약 75%도 3분기에 선반영
(잔여분은 4분기 반영). 일회성 요인을 제외하면 이익 체력은 견조. 
 동사의 조선 부문 매출액은 4조 3,988억원(+44.0%yoy), 영업이익은 
6,463억원(+62.8%yoy, OPM 14.7%)을 기록. 상선 OPM은 14.7%로 
2Q26(17.8%) 대비 하락했으나 대부분 외생변수 영향. 3Q26 대형선 매출 
중 2023년 수주분 비중은 45%, 2024~2025년 수주분은 51%. 고선가 
물량의 매출 비중이 높아지는 2027년까지 수익성 개선 여력은 충분.  
 9월 말 누적 상선 수주는 138.0억달러로 연간 목표의 120%, 엔진기계는 
41.4억달러로 155%를 달성했으나 함정(13%)과 해양(5%)은 부진. LNG선
은 중국 조선소의 저가 수주와 발주 둔화로 선가가 일부 하락. 선주들이 현 
선가를 저점으로 인식하며 발주 협의를 재개하는 분위기. 북미 LNG 프로젝
트 FID가 이어지고 있어 2027년 LNG선 발주 회복 기대. 
 함정 부문은 매출 2,570억원(-14.6%qoq)으로 줄었으나, 수출 비중 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선으로 영업이익 386억원(+114%qoq, OPM 15.0%)을 기록. 
해양플랜트는 매출액 3,799억원(+35.3%yoy), 영업이익 1,265억원(OPM 
33.3%). C/O 180억원을 제외해도 Ruya 공정 본격화와 Trion 고수익 물량
으로 높은 수익성 유지. 2027년 일부 프로젝트 인도로 매출 감소가 예상되
나, LNG선·블록 물량 투입으로 고정비 부담을 완화할 계획. 신규 해상변전
소(OSS) 계약을 앞두고 있으며, 2027년 초 추가 수주 발표도 기대. 
 주목할 부분은 엔진기계. 영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)으로 높은 D/F 
엔진 비중을 바탕으로 견조한 수익성 유지. 육상발전용 힘센엔진은 올해   
3GW를 확보하며 2029년까지 계획 캐파 수준의 물량을 이미 채움. 생산 
예상 물량은 2028년 약 1.5GW, 2029년 약 3GW이며, 신규 공장 구축 이
후 연간 생산능력은 약 4GW로 확대 예정. 북미 Hyperscaler와의 직접 협
상이 늘고 있으며, 힘센엔진을 활용한 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)로 FDC 시장
까지 대응 가능. AI 데이터센터 전력 수요가 2028년 이후 새로운 이익 축. 
 미국에서는 Kiewit과 합작법인 설립 계약 체결. 군수지원함 등 지원함 사업
부터 추진하며, 현지 조선소 건설 과정에서 HII에 선박 블록을 공급하는 방
안도 협의 중. 구체적인 투자계획은 2026년 말까지 확정 목표. 국내에서는 
핵추진잠수함 초기 예산이 2027년 반영되며 동사도 사업 참여를 준비 중. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 61만원을 제시. 고선가 물량 매출 확
대와 육상발전용 엔진 성장으로 피크아웃을 이겨낼 굴지의 조선사.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

## Document 273: AI“大到不能倒”了？特朗普亲自营救黄仁勋，被指是“现代水门事件”！

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI“大到不能倒”了？特朗普亲自营救黄仁勋，被指是“现代水门事件”！.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI 产业已深度嵌⼊美国经济，甚⾄到了 “ ⼤⽽不能倒 ” 的地步。 AI 抛售引发衰退恐慌，促使特
朗普政府施压英伟达董事会、⼒挺⻩仁勋复职，推动英伟达股价创纪录暴涨。但这场救市
⾏动正引发国会调查，被指具有 “ ⽔⻔事件 ” 特征，政治⻛险与 AI 估值隐患仍未消退。
摘要：
特朗普政府对英伟达董事会施压、推动⻩仁勋复职⼀事，正从⼀场市场救援⾏动演变为潜在的
政治危机。
据 Fox Business 记者 Charles Gasparino 援引知情⼈⼠消息，促使财政部⻓⻉森特转变⽴场、⼒
挺⻩仁勋复职的核⼼动因，是对经济衰退的恐惧 ——AI 股票的抛售潮令主要投资者向⽩宫周边
⼈⼠传递警告，称美国陷⼊衰退的概率 " 接近 100%" 。⻩仁勋复职消息公布后，英伟达单⽇暴涨
逾 34% ，创 2000 年以来最⼤单⽇涨幅，市值单⽇增加逾 1.1 万亿美元。
然⽽，Gasparino 将特朗普的这⼀操作称为 " 现代版⽔⻔事件 " 。1972 年美国总统⼤选期间，尼
克松竞选团队成员潜⼊⺠主党总部安装窃听器并被捕。此后，⽩宫掩盖真相、阻挠调查的⾏为

曝光，最终迫使尼克松于 1974 年辞职，成为美国历史上⾸位辞职的总统。
Gasparino 警告称国会调查已在路上，⺠主党⼈正将 " ⻩仁勋事件 " 纳⼊弹劾特朗普的议程，短期
市场提振背后潜伏着巨⼤的政治与市场⻛险。
救市逻辑：衰退警告促使⽩宫转向
据 Gasparino 报道，在上周 AI 股票遭到重挫后，主要投资者向⽩宫周边⼈⼠传递警告，认为 AI 交
易的平仓将冲击 " 整个科技⽣态系统 " 的⽀出，经济衰退概率 " 接近 100%" 。这⼀警告被视为⻉森
特急转弯的 " 主要原因 "—— 就在⼋天前，⻉森特还曾要求英伟达董事会在公司外部寻找⻩仁勋的
继任者，⽽到了周⽇，他已将所谓的间谍⻛险斥为 " 某种程度上的⽆中⽣有 " 。
Nomura 分析师 Charlie McElligott 在周⼀给客⼾的报告中指出，AI 与股市已 " ⼤到不能倒 "。他描
述了复职消息触发的机械式市场反应： " 对冲头⼨被强制平仓，空头遭到⽌损，标普期货被拉
爆，市场疯狂追涨。 "
穆迪⾸席经济学家 Mark Zandi 从宏观视⻆提供了另⼀维度的注脚。他估计，今年美国实际 GDP

增⻓中，最多有三分之⼀可归因于 AI 产业繁荣，并表⽰：
Coherence Credit Strategies 的 Sal Naro 向彭博表⽰，这代表了华尔街的共识判断：
市场反弹：机械买盘有限，真实买家仍待⼊场
英伟达单⽇涨幅超 34% 为市场提供了强⼼剂，但分析⼈⼠警告，技术性反弹⼒度有其天花板。
"AI 正在驱动整个经济列⻋。如果 AI 脱轨，整个经济也将随之受到冲击。 "
" 全球 AI 繁荣已经⼤到不容失败。 "
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

据 Nomura 模型测算，杠杆 ETF 与趋势跟踪基⾦合计形成约 600 亿美元的被动强制买盘。但⼀旦
标普 500 指数突破 7043 点，商品交易顾问（ CTA ）持仓将回归平仓状态，在指数触及 7625 点之
前不再有新增买盘压⼒。此后，市场能否持续上涨，将完全依赖真实资⾦的主动介⼊。
Goldman Sachs 分析师 Anton Tran 在其流动性报告中措辞克制： " 我还不确定市场的命运⼏乎等
同于⼀个⼈的命运，究竟是好事还是坏事。不过，我们并不挑剔。 "
Evercore ISI 的 TMT 交易团队态度则更为明确，其周⼀报告认为：
政治⻛险：国会调查逼近， " ⽔⻔ " 阴影挥之不去
Gasparino 的担忧并不⽌于市场层⾯。他将特朗普此番操作定性为具有 " ⽔⻔事件 " 特征 —— 通过
向董事会施压、在政治问题上 " ⼤做⽂章 " ，为⻩仁勋复职铺路，⽽这⼀路径本⾝已引发反弹。
⻩仁勋将于 12 ⽉ 1 ⽇接受三个众议院委员会的闭⻔录⾳听证，英伟达总法律顾问 Tim Teter 随后于

12 ⽉ 2 ⽇出席。⺠主党⼈要求调阅⻩仁勋与特朗普之间通话的所有记录，⽽特朗普本⼈在公开场
合炫耀⾃⼰已说服董事会，实际上已将这些通话置于调查的核⼼。
Semafor 主编 Ben Smith 认同 Gasparino 对华盛顿政治⾛向的判断；保守派评论⼈ Erick Erickson
亦表⽰，此事 " 将成为⼀个⼤问题，尤其是如果⻩仁勋或 Lutnick 的⼦⼥存在商业利益关联 " 。
英伟达董事会本⾝也未将此事视为已了结：董事会已成⽴委员会，正在聘任⾸席合规官，并表
⽰对该事件的内部调查将持续⾄结案。
⻓期隐患：⻩仁勋回归能否真正⽌住跌势？
⽆论政治如何演变，市场观察⼈⼠对⻩仁勋复职能否从根本上扭转 AI 估值修正存有保留。
Gasparino 本⼈对这⼀救市逻辑持审慎态度，他称此举是 " ⼀次⼤胆的策略，或许在强⼤合⼒的
作⽤下奏效 " ，但同时警告 " ⻛险正在快速积累 " ：
市场策略师 Gary Kaltbaum 对衰退警告的回应更加直⽩：
Gasparino 还转述了市场流传的两种解读：⼀是特朗普此举为记者奉上了 " ⽔⻔事件以来最好的

新闻素材 " ；⼆是⻩仁勋复职不过是在 "AI 交易⻓期已废 " 的背景下，为持仓股东提供了⼀个退出
流动性窗⼝。对此， Gasparino 表⽰： " 我认为两者皆有。 "
以美国家庭持有的股票资产接近 GDP 的 200% 这⼀背景衡量，市场能否在技术性反弹耗尽之后获
得真实资⾦的接⼒，或许才是这场政治与市场博弈最终成败的真正试⾦⽯。
" 当 AI 基础设施建设的历史被书写时，今天理应拥有独⽴的⼀章。这实际上是 AI 产业的第⼀次救
助⾏动 —— 救助的不是某家公司的资产负债表，⽽是⽀撑着⼀场万亿美元级产业建设的底层架

## Document 310: Chipping in_ how chips have become a huge driver of the dollar (2026-11-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chipping in_ how chips have become a huge driver of the dollar (2026-11-16).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Chipping in: how chips have  
become a huge driver of the dollar

1 
Authors 
Mallika Sachdeva 
Strategist

November 16, 2026 
What do chips have to do with a new era of dollar dominance? As we discuss in 
this paper – a great deal. Chips are not only critical to the future of AI but have 
recently emerged as a huge driver of global trade and capital flows. 
It is well known that the US is dominant in upstream chip technology and 
downstream compute. This note delves deeper into the workings of the middle of 
the chain where flows have been reinforcing the dollar’s global role. The 
manufacturing of chips largely takes place in Asia where chip-related trade 
surpluses have ballooned. As we show, these surpluses are finding their way 
back to the US via investments in US capital markets. 
Asian chip surpluses are not being recycled into US Treasuries like petrodollar 
surpluses were, but into US equities. Foreign inflows into US equities have 
crossed USD1tn a year and become the mainstay of US deficit funding. The 
biggest source of increase over the past year has come from Asia. 
Equity flows have not just been supporting the US dollar but have provided 
financing to the companies driving the AI buildout and chips boom itself. This 
introduces forms of circularity and potential instability to global flows. Moreover, 
equities are a much riskier asset class than bonds – US stocks are three times as 
volatile as Treasuries, with tech stocks up to ten times as much. Growing dollar 
dependence on inherently riskier capital should spill into higher US dollar 
volatility over time. 
The rise of chips as a driver of dollar dominance can be seen as a symptom of 
change in the ultimate source of US power - from geopolitical to technological 
leadership. This power is increasingly concentrated in the private sector with 
dollar supportive flows intermediated via private balance sheets. 
Understanding this new framework and its risks is even more important given 
rapid shifts in news flow around AI, from the US political climate, high-stakes 
capital market raisings, potential tightening in export controls, and the highly 
consequential technology race with China.

### Page 2

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

The rise of the chip dollar 
For the first time in modern history, global trade in semiconductors now 
exceeds trade in oil and gas (Figure 1) based on the four quarters of global 
exports till Q2-2026. More relevant from a currency perspective, is that chip 
surpluses in Asia ex-China have caught up to petroleum-related surpluses in the 
Gulf (Figure 2).  Why does this matter and what does this reflect?

Figure 1:  Global trade in chips has overtaken trade in 
oil & gas for the first time in history

Figure 2:  Asia (ex-China)’s chip surpluses are now as 
large as the Gulf’s petroleum-related surpluses

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, ITC Trade Map; Note: Oil & Gas are HS Codes 2709, 
2710, 2711.; Semiconductors is HS 8542

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, ITC Trade Map; Note: Oil & Gas are HS 2709, 2710, 
2711.; Semiconductors is HS 8542; Asia ex-China is Japan, Korea, Taiwan & ASEAN

We argue that the petrodollar is – rather quickly - being superseded by the chip-
dollar. This is both literal in terms of global surplus accumulation, but also 
archetypal. In other words, the petrodollar and chip-dollar are archetypes for two 
different states of the world, and for alternative foundations of dollar dominance.  
The petrodollar regime can be understood as one where US geopolitical 
leadership – over global security, international rules, and free trade – motivated 
global savings to be reinvested in the US sovereign via Treasury holdings. This 
cheapened funding for the US government which in turn supported the financing 
of US public goods provision.  
The emerging chip-dollar regime is one where US technology leadership is 
motivating global savings to be reinvested in US companies – providing funding 
for the US corporate sector to make enormous investments in new technology 
such as AI.  
The transition from the petrodollar to the chip-dollar is therefore a 
manifestation of change in the US’ leadership style - from geopolitical to 
technology leadership. This is not only changing the nature of global imbalances 
and capital flow recycling but is reshaping the foundations of dollar dominance.  
Revisiting the petrodollar regime  
After the 1971 Nixon shock, when the dollar’s convertibility to gold was 
terminated, we moved into a fully fiat currency world. The US dollar since then 
has been backed only by the fiscal credibility of the US government and the 
willingness of the world to finance US deficits by buying US assets.  
16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 3

Deutsche Bank AG Page 3

The 1974 petrodollar deal provided a crucial pillar of support for the dollar in its 
fiat infancy and sowed the seeds for a post-Bretton Woods regime in which US 
geopolitical leadership supported dollar dominance.  In 1974, in exchange for US 
security and military guarantees, Saudi Arabia agreed to price and invoice oil sales 
in USD. These USD denominated oil surpluses were then reinvested in US 
Treasuries. This was soon replicated across the Gulf. The 1990s and 2000s saw 
huge growth in commodity and manufacturing surpluses around the world. These 
were arguably supported by the US’s global backstop for trade and security – with 
surpluses saved in USD and recycled into US Treasuries via public balance sheets, 
in what can be considered a petrodollar-plus regime.  
We wrote earlier in the year how the Iran war could undermine the critical 
foundations of the petrodollar system by challenging the US security umbrella 
and shifting perceptions around US geopolitical leadership. We maintain that 
these changes are underway. But what has become apparent since then is that 
something else has been rising to take the petrodollar’s place in support of the 
dollar.  
The chip dollar is working differently  
While petrodollar surpluses were recycled into US Treasuries, chips surpluses 
are being recycled very differently.  
We focus on the chip surpluses being built in Asia ex-China. We note that China 
runs a very large semiconductor deficit, despite having the world’s largest overall 
trade surplus - something we will return to later. Asia’s chip surpluses are thus 
primarily hosted in Korea and Taiwan which are large semiconductor 
manufacturers, to a lesser extent in Japan which produces key materials, and 
ASEAN-10 which plays a role in assembly, packaging and testing.  
To explore how chip dollar surpluses are being recycled, we zoom in on South 
Korea – the epitome of the boom in chips trade.  
Korea’s semiconductor exports have grown over 200% YoY this year, with Korean 
chipmakers dominating in the manufacture of memory chips critical to AI 
acceleration. As Figure 3 shows, Korea’s semiconductor trade surplus has 
ballooned to close to USD250bn with 100% of Korea’s overall trade surplus now 
on account of chips alone.  
What is striking is that Korea’s chip surplus is being recycled abroad entirely via 
equities. Foreign sales of Korean equities have accounted for half the capital 
outflows in the past year, while Korean purchases of overseas equities account 
for the other half (Figure 3). Korean investments in overseas equities have been 
five times more than Korean purchases of overseas bonds in the last two years. In 
short, chip surpluses are being saved in global equities, not debt. 
The primary destination of Korea’s overseas equity purchases has been the US. 
While the US accounts for around 50% of global equity market capitalization, we 
note it is 70% of Korea’s overseas equity holdings (IMF CPIS). The US bias is even 
more exaggerated for Korean retail investors – a group empowered by access to 
financial technology. Korean households have 90% of their equity exposure in the 
US (Figure 4). It appears that North Asian capital is flowing back to the very US 
technology companies that are driving the chip surpluses themselves.

16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 4

Deutsche Bank AG Page 4

Figure 3:  Asian chip surpluses are being recycled into 
US equities – not US Treasuries like petrodollars were

Figure 4: 70% of Korea’s equity exposure abroad is to 
the US, and that figure is more than 90% for households

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, CEIC; Note: The semiconductor trade balance here is 
measured using Korea’s Customs Service classification for Semiconductors and 
equipment for semiconductor manufacturing

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics; Note: Data based on IMF’s 
Coordinated Portfolio Investment Survey as of end-2025

The reach for US equities by Asian retail is not just happening in Korea. In Japan, 
there have been significant allocations to US focused ETFs, encouraged by the 
NISA scheme. And as we have argued in our earlier paper, the US’s embrace of 
asset tokenization and the upcoming shift by US exchanges to 23/5 trading could 
further embolden and enable retail investors to access US equities. We could thus 
just be at the beginning of this new trend. Countries in North Asia like Korea and 
Japan not only have large savings pools, but also open capital accounts.  
This shift has shown up dramatically on the US capital account. Foreign 
purchases of US equities have exceeded USD1tn over the past year (Figure 5). 
Interestingly, most of the increase in purchases has come from Asia (Figure 6). 
Notably, foreign purchases of US equities are now more than double US Treasury 
allocations, which appear in relative decline. Even foreign purchases of US 
corporate bonds now exceed US Treasuries. This is notable considering the ramp 
up in hyper scaler issuance.

Figure 5: Foreign flows into US stocks now exceed 
flows into US Treasuries by a significant margin

Figure 6: Most of the increase in US equity purchases 
over the past year has come from Asia

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics (TICS data)

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics (TICS data)

16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 5

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

Chips are the oil of the new economy  
The power of having oil priced and paid for in dollars was not just the recycling 
of petrodollar surpluses, but the value chain impact of having a key upstream 
resource dollarized. Oil is a critical input to almost every industrial process via 
petrochemicals – it is found in everything from plastic to paints, tires to 
cosmetics. It also plays a role in moving traded goods around the world as the 
transport fuel for ships. We would argue that having oil priced in dollars played a 
role in dollarizing global trade as companies looked to match the currency of 
downstream receivables to the upstream costs of oil. This is consistent with the 
Dominant Currency Paradigm (Gopinath et al, 2020).  
Chips arguably play a similar role in the digital and consumer economy as oil has 
played in the industrial economy. Chips are inputs to everything from consumer 
electronics, to automobiles, computers, communication devices, and most 
recently the bedrock of modern AI systems.  
Indeed, chips make up the lion’s share of spending in the AI buildout. Analysis by 
Epoch AI found that servers represented about 60% of the annualized cost of an 
indicative 1GW data centre in the US (Figure 7). According to the Semiconductor 
Industry Association (SIA), chips represent 95% of an AI server rack's value. In 
simple terms therefore, we can say chips make up over 50% of total capital 
expenditures for an AI data centre. Bloomberg Intelligence estimates hyper-
scaler capital expenditure commitments of close to USD8tn out to 2031 (Figure 
8), which would suggest a further USD4tn of chips demand from AI alone.

Figure 7: More than half the cost of the AI data centre 
build out is going towards chips

Figure 8:  Capital expenditure in the AI buildout may 
still have a long way to go

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Epoch AI; * Note: Based on a stylized model of a US 
hyperscaler AI data center that has 1 GW capacity. CapEx is converted to annual costs 
over each asset’s lifespan, assuming 5 years for IT equipment like chips.

Source: Deutsche Bank Research

To be sure, the market will remain sensitive to shifts in news flow and risks that 
the AI data centre buildout might slow whether due to the changing political 
climate in the US, calls for growing public oversight and safety monitoring of the 
industry, or a renewed focus on export controls.  
There are also open questions as to whether the boom in chips demand will be 
“one-off” as capacity is built or will be ongoing like with oil. Oil is a huge source of 
ongoing dollar flows primarily because of how consistent and trade-intensive it 
16 November 2026
 FX Special Report

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank AG Page 6

is: 100 million barrels are consumed daily with 70% of them being traded. We note 
that the replacement rate of chips in an average data centre is estimated at 3-7 
years, which suggests there could be ongoing demand even once the projected 
capex buildout reaches its zenith. But it remains to be seen whether the chips 
dollar can generate the type of enduring and stable recycling that the petrodollar 
has generated. 
Staying with the comparison between the petrodollar and chip-dollar, arguably it 
was US military presence in the world’s largest oil producing region in the 1970s 
– the Gulf - that gave the US control over the pricing of oil. What is it that gives 
the US control over the pricing and trade in chips? 
US control over chips: technology, economic rents, and supply chains   
The integrated circuit was invented in the US in 1958 with the microchip industry 
flourishing in California to become the well-known Silicon Valley. It is hard to 
overstate the continued dominance of the US in chips since then. Work by Sean 
Kenji Starrs of Kings College London maps the present-day dominance in 
compelling terms1. 
While most chip manufacturing happens in Asia – which is where the trade 
surpluses discussed above are being accumulated – most of the economic rents 
to chip-making accrues to US companies. Around 50% of the value in the 
semiconductor supply chain is in the design process where US companies control 
87% of the revenue (Starrs). The US has a near monopoly on the electronic design 
automation (EDA) software necessary for design, and controls around 60% of the 
revenue in semiconductor manufacturing equipment without which chips cannot 
be made. Materials, manufacturing, packaging and testing of chips happen 
outside the US, but these are worth only around 30% of the estimated value of 
the industry. Indeed, Starrs finds that 70% of the profits from the semiconductor 
industry flow to US firms (Figure 9).  
Furthermore, we note that all key countries involved in the supply chain of 
advanced chips remain US aligned. Japan plays an important role in materials, 
while Taiwan and Korea dominate in production (foundries). Importantly each of 
these North Asian players leans geopolitically towards the US over China. The US 
has arguably been less vocal about withdrawing economic support for security in 
North Asia than with NATO. One can thus posit the existence of a US-Asia chip-
dollar bloc. We note that the Netherlands has a monopoly on EUV lithography 
machines required to make the most advanced chips, but production of these 
machines in turn have a dependency on a US optical equipment firm helping 
grant the US crucial leverage even here.  
The potent combination of US control over key upstream technologies and 
intellectual property, alongside a supply chain located in US allies has given the 
US enormous control over chips. In recent years, the US has actively exercised 
this power via export controls to limit China’s access to US advanced chips and 
technologies. This has been the subject of books from Chip War by Chris Miller to 
Chokepoints by Edward Fishman. The importance of ensuring the integrity of US 
export controls was made apparent in recent comments by US Trade 
Representative Greer who noted that the US wants the “crown jewels” of the 
semiconductor industry to remain in the hands of the US or its allies. The US

1 Starrs, Sean Kenji. 2025. “Ringfencing Globalization: US Structural Power, Techno-Nationalism & Chinese Dependent 
Integration.” In Chapter 8 in Globalization in a Turbulent Era 
16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 7

Deutsche Bank AG Page 7

government has arguably had significant influence over the private sector sale of 
US chips technology and may double down on this after recent events at Nvidia 
to limit both direct access to US chips and the leasing of global data centers built 
with US chips to non-US allies (CNBC).

Figure 9: The US is very dominant in chips – controlling 
key upstream technology and an enormous part of the 
market value of the industry

Figure 10: China continues to run a large trade deficit in 
semiconductors – notable given China’s over USD2tn 
manufacturing goods surplus

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Starrs, Sean Kenji. 2025. “Ringfencing Globalization: 
US Structural Power, Techno-Nationalism & Chinese Dependent Integration.” In 
Chapter 8 in Globalization in a Turbulent Er; * Data for 2024 based on 40 chip 
companies in the Forbes 2000 ^ Data for 2000 based on 29 companies

Source: Deutsche Bank Research, ITC Trade Map (HS 8542)

Where does China fit in with all this?  
This note has looked at key changes in the industrial drivers of global imbalances 
(from fossil fuels to chips) and capital flows to the US (from debt to equities). 
Where does China – with the world’s biggest trade surplus – fit in?  
China now runs an over USD2 trillion manufactured goods surplus per year but 
continues to run a persistent semiconductor deficit (Figure 10). While US export 
controls limit access to the most advanced computing chips, China continues to 
import large volumes of memory and logic chips. Restrictions on access to 
advanced manufacturing equipment, particularly EUV lithography, have 
constrained China’s advances in domestic chip-making at the technological 
frontier, although greater self-sufficiency remains a major strategic priority. We 
note that the other drag on China’s trade position is commodities, mostly fossil 
fuels. Notably therefore, the remaining holes in China’s trade surplus are 
dominated by chip-dollar and petrodollar imports. In a forthcoming paper, we will 
look at China’s gains in renewable energy, new energy exports, and electrification, 
examining how these could emerge as long-term sources of power in both the AI 
race, and in increasing China’s independence from the dollar.  
The recycling of China’s manufactured goods surplus remains the topic of 
considerable debate. China’s surplus is in many ways the mirror of the US deficit, 
albeit with growing contribution from Europe, and increasingly intermediated 
through the Global South. There remains a school of thought that these Chinese 
savings find their way to the US through state banks and custodian balance 
sheets. But we note important points of difference with the chip dollar recycling 
we have discussed.  Nearly 50% of the net international assets held by Korea, 
16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

### Page 8

Deutsche Bank AG Page 8

Japan and Taiwan are in portfolio investments – namely equities and bonds. For 
China, this number is less than 20%. This is consistent with China’s capital account 
restrictions on private capital, as well as a growing geopolitical preference to limit 
exposure to US capital markets. Instead, China holds a much greater share of 
direct and other assets – reflecting growth in Chinese state-directed lending and 
acquisition of hard assets particularly in the Global South. On public reserve 
balance sheets, we note a growing preference for gold over dollar assets. Finally, 
we note a shift towards conversion of China’s surpluses, showing up in a stronger 
RMB and lower domestic rates.  
Bringing it together 
In the age of AI, it is well known that the US is dominant in both upstream chip 
technology as well as downstream computing. The US designs and controls 
supply chains for the world’s most advanced chips and is home to 10 times as 
many data centers as any other country (Stanford HAI). Both are sources of US 
power. This note has delved deeper into the workings of the middle of the chain 
where new flows are reinforcing the dollar’s global role in trade and capital flows. 
The manufacturing of high-end chips takes place in Asia ex-China and generates 
large trade surpluses there. This money is finding its way back to the US via 
Asian investments in US equities and corporate bonds. These capital flows are 
both funding the US trade deficit and providing financing to US companies that 
are driving the AI boom. Chips are thus ensuring the continued dominance of 
the dollar in global trade, payments, capital markets, and private sector savings, 
as well as supporting the US AI buildout seen as crucial to the future.  
There is an element of circularity to be conscious of: equity flows and financing 
for US AI related stocks are being funded out of Asian chip-related surpluses 
which are themselves being boosted by demand from US AI companies. This 
could introduce instability in global flows should demand be reassessed. For 
instance, if consumer demand for compute does not keep up with costly 
investments in supply, this would not just reduce earnings estimates for AI 
companies, but ultimately chip demand. One can see how this would challenge 
foreign equity financing for the US from both a push and pull perspective.  
Separately, as we noted in our last paper, proliferation of Chinese open-source 
models could also undermine the profitability of US companies and the 
attractiveness of US markets to global capital.  
The fact that the US dollar has become more dependent on foreign equity 
inflows than US Treasuries should make it inherently more volatile and exposed. 
Equities by nature are a riskier asset class than bonds. Recent realized volatility in 
S&P500 has been 3 times as much as in a US 10Y Treasuries. NASDAQ volatility is 
double that of the S&P and the Philly Semiconductor Index more than triple.  That 
US deficit financing is now dependent on capital flows into a more volatile asset 
class should spill over into currency volatility in time.  
In sum, US dollar dominance is now dependent on US technology leadership 
driven by private companies, and the flows underpinning the dollar are 
increasingly being intermediated on private sector balance sheets. A dollar driven 
by chips, AI and private capital connections should be inherently riskier.  
16 November 2026 
FX Special Report

Deutsche Bank AG Page 9

Investor selling weighs on copper
Copper is on track for one of its steepest weekly declines in a
decade.
We think the speed of the sell-oﬀ partly reﬂects an unwind of bullish
investor positioning. Both LME and COMEX net length have fallen
sharply from recent highs, while positioning across the wider
metals complex has also become less bullish.

sentiment, but it does not make copper's demand story dependent

Aluminium supply remains tight for now

Aluminium has fallen with the wider complex, but nearby supply
remains tight. The LME cash-to-three-month spread has moved

Gold feels the pressure from higher rates

## Document 376: New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-24).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• As polls suggested, the election was a nailbiter
that has not yet been resolved. Election night
results show a lead to the centre-right on
paper in a significantly enlarged parliament.
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First
and ACT won 67 seats. The centre-left bloc
of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti Māori won 57
seats. There was a swing away from National
to its coalition partners while the centre left
grouping picked up a few seats.

• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling
and will enter parliament with at least 7 seats,
reducing what could have been a larger swing
to the centre-left grouping.
• The size of parliament will expand by around 9
seats as National held onto a large share of its
electorate votes, entitling it to more seats than
implied by its party vote share. As a result,
a bloc would require 66 seats to command a
majority in parliament.
• Several senior National ministers have not

been re-elected, including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. She will continue in a caretaker
role but will need to be replaced in any new
centre-right government.
• Significant uncertainty about the result exists.
National might lose perhaps two marginal
electorates in the final analysis. There are
400,000 special votes to count which may
shift the list seat allocations as these tend
to reduce the share of the centre-right bloc
by 1.6-1.7ppts. Hence, when all is said and

done, the centre-right’s 10 seat advantage on
election night could reduce by 1-4 seats.
• Should this play out the centre-right would not
have sufficient seats to reach a majority in an
enlarged house and would require the support
of the Opportunity Party to govern.
• The final election outcome will not be
known until 27 November. Serious coalition
negotiations likely won’t begin until then
and delays could occur. Should recounts be
required, some further delay is possible.

• Based on these results, we see a higher
probability of a centre-right government
ultimately emerging that likely will require
the support of the Opportunity Party in some
form. The Opportunity Party likely won’t enter
Cabinet but may offer support on confidence
and supply in exchange for policy concessions.
• But a centre-left government can’t be ruled
out if special votes heavily favour the centre
left bloc, and especially if an agreement
between the Opportunity Party and the centre-

right parties can’t be reached.
• Regardless of the final count, the results
suggest a government with weak support and
ongoing vulnerabilities. Hence the prospect of
controversial large changes may be modest.
For a centre-right government that might
limit the extent of fiscal consolidation, while
the centre left’s plans for larger scale tax and
spending initiatives could be trimmed back.
• A centre-right government won’t change the
RBNZ mandate while a centre-left government

likely still will. A new finance minister will be
required with the possibility of ACT leader
David Seymour taking on that role.
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
New Zealand 2026 General Election:
Preliminary results and next steps.
9 Nov 2026 |  Westpac Economics Team | westpac.co.nz/economics | economics@westpac.co.nz
Kelly Eckhold, Chief Economist | +64 9 348 9382 | +64 21 786 758 | kelly.eckhold@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
The result.
The preliminary results of the 2026 General Election,
based on the election night count, are set out in the table
above. In summary:
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First and ACT
won 67 seats.
• The centre-left bloc of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti
Māori won 57 seats.
• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling and will
enter parliament with 7 seats.
These results indicate a swing away from National to its

coalition partners while the centre-left grouping picked
up a few seats. The big gainer was the Opportunity Party
who perhaps took a big share of what otherwise could
have been a larger swing to the centre-left grouping.
The size of parliament will expand by around nine seats,
as National held onto a large share of its electorate
votes and hence has more seats than it would have been
entitled to based on its party vote share. As a result, a
governing bloc would require 66 seats to command a

majority in parliament.
Several of the electorate seats are very tight (see Table
2), so election night majorities could easily be overturned
once special votes are counted. Of particular relevance
is the seat of Wairarapa, where the National candidate is
leading the Labour candidate by just 139 votes. If Labour
were to win that seat (specials favoured Labour in 2020
but National in 2023), all else equal the centre-right seat
count would decline to 66 seats in what would become

a 130-seat Parliament – just enough to command a
majority. But if either NZ First or ACT were to lose a single
party vote seat to the left bloc once specials are counted
then that majority would disappear.
On that score, historically the election night results have
tended to overstate the support of the centre-right bloc
and understate the support for parties on the left of
the political spectrum. For example, over the previous
three elections (2017, 2020, 2023), the current centre-

right bloc’s vote share has declined 1.9%, 1.7% and 1.6%
respectively following the counting of special votes, with
the National Party losing 2 seats on each occasion once
the official results were declared (Labour, the Greens and
Te Pāti Māori have benefited). On this occasion, given its
overhang, a lower party vote share would not lower the
National Party’s final vote count, although it could alter
the size of its overhang and thus the number of seats

required to command a majority. In recent elections both
NZ First and ACT have seen their vote shares decline
following the counting of special votes.
The process from here.
Official results.
The official results will be declared on 27 November,
adding the results of an estimated 400,000 special votes
(with around a quarter of these cast overseas) to the
2.41m votes counted on election night.
We expect discussions between potential coalition
partners will begin immediately to start sketching out the

parameters of a coalition agreement. But given the tight
margins of the current vote count, no firm positions will
be agreed until the results are declared.
The writ and recounts.
After 27 November, the writ is scheduled to be returned
on 3 December – formally certifying who has been
elected. However, that date is subject to any recounts

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
that might be requested. Parliament is required to meet
no later than 14 January (six weeks after the return of the
writ), providing an opportunity for a new government
to test that it has the confidence of the House. It is
worth noting that Parliament can be summoned and
begin functioning even if coalition negotiations are still
occurring. If no coalition or confidence-and-supply
arrangement has been reached by then, negotiations can

simply continue and the incumbent Government remains
in office under the caretaker convention (Ministers in
the outgoing government retain executive authority
temporarily, even if they are no longer MPs).
Given that the Pre-Election Economic and Fiscal
Update was released in late September there is no legal
requirement for the Treasury to publish a Half Year
Economic and Fiscal Update (HYEFU) this year. And given
that it will be some weeks before a government is formed,

it seems unlikely that there will be a government in place
that would request that a HYEFU be produced despite
there being no statutory requirement (certainly there
would be little time to incorporate policy changes that
fall out of new coalition agreements, thus diminishing
its value). Once a government is formed, it will have an
opportunity to lay out its immediate priorities in the
Budget Policy Statement (BPS), which by law needs to
be released before the end of March next year. The new

government could decide to release an interim fiscal
update with the BPS if that was thought necessary.
Potential coalition outcomes.
We see three main likely outcomes:
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First.
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First with the support of the Opportunity Party
on confidence and supply.
• Some coalition of Labour, the Greens, Opportunity and
Te Pāti Māori, potentially with Opportunity and Te Pāti

Māori sitting outside the cabinet.
Our preview of the election discussed the policies of
the main political parties, while the figure on page 5
highlights some points of commonality with the centre-
right and centre-left blocs.
National, ACT and NZ First share a broadly pro-
development and deregulatory agenda, including support
for greater resource development, fewer regulatory
barriers and compulsory or expanded KiwiSaver. Beyond
that common core, National and ACT align particularly

closely on lower taxes and deregulation, while National
and NZ First share an emphasis on infrastructure, regional
development and law and order. ACT and NZ First favour
tighter immigration settings and tighter welfare policies.
The differences are clearest at the edges: ACT advocates
deeper tax cuts, smaller government and more market-
led provision, while NZ First places greater emphasis
on economic sovereignty, regional development and
targeted support for working New Zealanders. National

occupies more of the middle ground, combining fiscal
discipline and a pro-business agenda with increased
infrastructure and housing supply.
The Opportunity Party presents itself as a middle
alternative that can work with the left or the right
groupings. They have five bottom line policies they say
they need to support any new government. These include
competition reform through enhanced Commerce
Commission powers, more restrictions on political
lobbyists and donors, a ten-year plan of investment in the

healthcare sector, an end to bottom trawling in inshore
areas and a phase out elsewhere and for the Government
to recommit to New Zealand’s Paris Agreement
obligations. Their success in meeting these bottom
lines with a new government and their flexibility around
these requirements will be key to their ability to form an
agreement with either side. There is better congruence
of the Opportunity Party’s policies with the left than the
right – so progress here will be critical to watch should

their support be required to form a new government. We
suspect they will be very motivated to avoid the need for
a new election via a hung parliament should push come
to shove.

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Labour, the Greens and Opportunity share support
for increased public and social investment, a broader
tax base, greater infrastructure investment, stronger
competition policy, cheaper public transport and a
more active role for government. Labour and the Greens
overlap particularly on higher taxation, public investment
and stronger climate and environmental policy, while
Labour and Opportunity share an emphasis on social

investment and higher public-service wages. The Greens
and Opportunity both favour broader taxation and
stronger climate action, although their preferred tax
instruments differ. Their distinctive priorities are also
clear: Labour places more weight on worker protections
and restoring the RBNZ’s dual mandate; the Greens on
environmental protection, progressive taxation and Te
Tiriti; and Opportunity on land-value taxation, housing
infrastructure, productivity, innovation and evidence-

based policy.

and differences.

PUBLISHED
November 11, 2026 8:32 p.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The PBOC argues—unpersuasively, I think—that it
doesn’t have any hidden foreign exchange reserves. I
would reframe that as hidden foreign currency assets,
as foreign currency lent out (or swapped out) with
domestic financial institutions is a classic way of
masking reserves.
Many in Asia—and some in the IMF—are inclined
to believe the PBOC. Fair enough, it is hard to

challenge an institution as large and powerful (in
some ways) as the PBOC.

### Page 3

3
But to convince, the PBOC really needs to provide
answers to three questions.
1. What explains the current gap between foreign
exchange settlement and the PBOC’s reserves?
Settlement is the purchase of foreign exchange in the
market by the PBOC and the state banks. It
technically implies taking an open foreign currency
position, as the foreign exchange is being bought with
renminbi.
Historically, it correlated with changes in the PBOC’s
balance sheet.

And some gaps before 2012 can be explained. In late
2005 and 2006, the PBOC did over $100 billion in
swaps with the state banks, who used the FX to buy
foreign bonds (portfolio debt outflows surged). Those

### Page 4

4
swaps were unwound after the global financial crisis,
and actually provided some of the foreign currency
that “funded” the China Investment Corporation
when it initially scaled up.
And in 2007 and 2008, the PBOC required that the
state banks hold FX against their domestic RMB
deposits (to keep the FX out of reserves; it was held
on deposit at the PBOC and shows up as “other
foreign assets” on the PBOC’s balance sheet and as
“other, other, short-term” in the balance of payments.*

After 2018, though, there are unexplained gaps
between the FX bought in settlement and the
PBOC’s balance sheet (technically cumulative
monthly changes to capture the fact that FX is held as
its initial CNY purchase price).
There was a net accumulation of FX outside the
PBOC’s balance sheet in 2020 and 2021, when the
CNY was under pressure to appreciate. There are
modest sales in 2023 and 2024 when there is
depreciation pressure. And there are large purchases

in 2025 and the first 9 months of 2026—total
purchases in settlement in the last 12 months (spot)
are $500 to 600 billion, while the increase in the
PBOC’s balance sheet is only around $50 billion over
the same period.

### Page 5

5
The gap over time is now $1 trillion. The PBOC
needs to be able to answer a simple question: who
holds this FX, and who holds the associated FX
market risk (given the FX has been purchased with
RMB).
The monthly pattern of growth in settlement strongly
suggests it is backdoor intervention; there are sales
when spot is weak (economically) relative to the fix,
and purchases when spot is either at the fix or just
stronger than the fix (the strong edge of the band

hasn’t been used). The pattern of purchases and sales
thus looks exactly like that of a central bank
maintaining a tight band around a (crawling) peg.
If the PBOC cannot credibly explain this gap, there
isn’t much more to discuss. The PBOC argues that it
hasn’t swapped FX with the state banks or put foreign
currency on deposit at the state banks. Fair enough.

### Page 6

6
But someone is buying in settlement; the PBOC
ought to know who…
2. Missing interest income
There is an obvious tell here—Bloomberg reported
back in 2022 that the PBOC transferred $158 billion
in accumulated interest income to the Ministry of
Finance as part of a special dividend to hold the
budget deficit down. Again, fair enough. But
absolutely nothing changed in the FX reserves held
on the PBOC’s balance sheet or in the balance of
payments.
That highlights a broader issue. Since the end of

2016, the PBOC’s balance sheet FX hasn’t really
changed.

### Page 7

7
Peer institutions generally have an upward slope to
their reserves as a result of “gains from reserve
management” or “reinvestment of interest income.” **
The Central Bank of China (Taipei) and Japan’s
Ministry of Finance are good examples.
The interest on ~$3 trillion in reserves should be
around $100 billion a year. A bit less during the “low-
for-long” era before the pandemic, when SAFE wasn’t
getting much income out of its euro portfolio. A bit

more now, when U.S. short-term rates are well above 3
percent and SAFE’s legacy agencies carry yields of 5
or 6 percent. $100 billion over 10 years is real money
—roughly a trillion dollars.
If SAFE has steadily been selling its interest income,
it should say so (the fact that BOP reserves have often
diverged from the PBOC balance sheet suggests a
more complex story).

### Page 8

8
Moreover, if SAFE has been transferring the FX to
one of China’s many sovereign wealth funds
(including those controlled by SAFE) or the policy
banks, it should say so.
But SAFE cannot say what you see is what we really
have if it cannot explain how it handles the interest
on its now $3.5 trillion in reserves.
3. The accounting for the PBOC’s entrusted loans
and other foreign currency claims on domestic
financial institutions, notably the policy banks

FX handed over to a sovereign wealth fund to manage
doesn’t appear in reserves. It isn’t a liquid reserve asset.
But it should appear elsewhere in the balance of
payments as either portfolio debt or portfolio equity.
And FX handed over to the policy banks through
mechanisms such as entrusted loans (or a simple
deposit or swap) doesn’t count as a foreign reserve—
the foreign currency claim is on a domestic financial
institution, and that isn’t considered a “foreign” asset.

There is no doubt that the PBOC has domestic
claims on China’s financial institutions. The use of
“entrusted” loans to fund the policy banks in 2010
was reported by Caixin and the Financial Times.

### Page 9

9
$95 billion in entrusted loans were converted into
equity in the CDB and China Exim back in 2015
(giving SAFE majority equity stakes in both
institutions through its Buttonwood investment
vehicle).
And the SAFE’s 2020 annual report contained a ten-
year progress report on the “diversified use” of China’s
FX (basically, the non-reserve use) which indicated
that SAFE continued to provide entrusted loans to
small, medium, and large Chinese financial

institutions.
Now perhaps these instruments have been unwound
—the China Development Corporation (per Stephen
Green, a friend) has reduced its foreign currency
balance sheet over the last few years. But that should
lead the foreign currency to flow back to SAFE
absent another use—and that increase in FX isn’t
showing up in the PBOC’s reserves.
In other words, SAFE should provide a transparent
accounting of the FX that has been bought but
transferred to other financial institutions and any

ongoing foreign currency exposure to the domestic
banking system (including the policy banks).
These aren’t big asks. The settlement gap is big and
obvious, interest income is a known issue, and SAFE
itself has reported its use of entrusted loans in the
past. There is no issue around the past use of swaps

### Page 10

10
either. A substantial share of the banks’ foreign
currency assets are funded domestically. It should be
possible for SAFE to explain the role, if any, it has
played in this funding.
There is, of course, another issue around China’s
balance of payments: the growth in foreign currency
external assets funded out of domestic foreign
currency deposits.
China argues that the growing foreign assets of its
state banks are simply a natural part of a growing

domestic financial system. But there is more to the
story.
Onshore FX deposits have tended to rise when
economics say they should not, and fall when the
economics say they should (offshore FX deposits
behave as expected). In 2020 and 2021, FX deposits

### Page 11

11
rose even when there was more yield on RMB
deposits.
And RMB deposits have continued to rise in 2025
and 2026 even with the RMB’s appreciation
offsetting the higher dollar deposit rate. This isn’t an
accident: banks just raised the interest rate that they
pay on dollar deposits to encourage exporters NOT to
convert their export proceeds into RMB and thus
take pressure off the PBOC (per Reuters).
In other words, the pattern of the banks’ foreign

currency asset growth suggests that they are offsetting
appreciation pressure by bidding for FX (or receiving
it from other state actors), not that FX flows into the
banks during periods when onshore residents worry
about depreciation.

### Page 12

12
Right now, the “settlement gap” is the most pressing
issue. FX deposit growth in excess of FX loan growth
can explain FX outflows of around $150 billion in the
four quarters to Q3. The FX outflows implied by
settlement reached $600 billion over the last four
quarters. Together they account for the bulk of
China’s reported $825 billion plus (I estimate a Q4
2025 to Q3 2026 surplus of around $900 billion) in
outflows implied by the reported current account.***

### Page 13

13
It goes without saying that this matters because the
weak RMB is the central reason for China’s massive
export outperformance since 2023—and that export
outperformance has continued in 2026.****
* I have been doing this a long time; I actually do
know most of the historical quirks in China’s balance

### Page 14

14
of payments data.
** The MoF can count a portion of its unrepatriated
gains from accumulated interest on its large reserves
against the budget deficit, but since the actual
interest is held abroad, it still needs to borrow
domestically against those reserves (domestic JGB
issuance and foreign reserves both go up).
*** My estimate of a larger “true” current account
comes from moving gold imports over to the financial
account, and from an assumption that undercounting

of the reinvested earnings of funds held offshore
explains the obvious problem in the investment
income account.
**** The spike in imports in Q1 wasn’t entirely real.
China was slow to adjust its import price index for
the rise in semiconductor/chip prices. This inflated
both import and export volumes in Q1. The rise in
gold imports this year also registers in the import
volume series—though the rise is best understood as
“importing” a financial asset.

### Page 15

15

Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and

Executive summary
上週我們提到接近年末，市場都會棄舊迎新尋找明年飆股及本夢⽐⾏情，主要

是針對營收及獲利仍有⼤幅增⻑潛⼒的公司，都會在獲利還沒爆發前提前先
跑，⽽造成  P/E 估值升級，尤其投資⼈在經歷 10-11 ⽉份的全球科技及半導體
產業股價⼤修正超過 30% 之後 ( 費半從歷史⾼點 14,655 回檔達 35% ），我們精
選了 20 家於 2027-2028 營收及獲利增⻑將明顯優於同業的台灣科技及半導體
公司（請參考之前報告：Time to ﬁnd 2027 soaring stocks），這次我們來
精選美國 20 家營收及獲利快速增⻑的科技及半導體公司，但加⼊了 adj EPS
yoy, 並⽤⽑利及營業利潤率變化來判斷營運槓桿差異，⽐較 rule of 40 預測
及⽐較未來 4 年 PEG 估值是否合理？

Jensen 從 Nvidia 離職後，我們相信  Nvidia 對於⼩型新興雲的財務挹注會⼤
幅降低）的產業及公司獲利模式⾄今仍存有極⼤不確定性及⾵險，所以在這次

PEG 估值偏⾼？這其中包括 5 家 AI 數據算⼒中⼼及系統組裝公司， 7 家 AI 運算及

AI ⾼速互聯晶⽚公司， 3 家光纖通訊雷射光相關公司， 2 家晶⽚測試設備公司，
